About Sripada Srivallabha Charitamrutam

🕉️ The Divine Subject

Sripada Srivallabha is the first incarnation of Dattatreya in Kali Yuga. Born circa 1320 CE in Pithapuram (East Godavari, Andhra Pradesh), He performed countless miracles and disappeared at Kuruvapuram (Krishna River island) in 1350 CE at age 30.

✍️ The Author

Shankar Bhatt — a Brahmin pilgrim from Karnataka who personally received Sripada's grace and was commanded by Him to write this biography. The entire book is Shankar Bhatt's first-person pilgrimage narrative across all 51 chapters.

📚 The Book

  • 51 chapters, 594 pages (English edition)
  • Official registers: 130+ persons, 90+ places
  • Brought to light in 2001 by Malladi Govinda Deekshitulu (33rd-gen descendant of Bapannarya)
  • Translated from Telugu; originally composed in divine revelation
  • Edition referenced: Dhanishtha Publications, Visakhapatnam (2001 Telugu / 2019 English). Official site: sripada-srivallabha.org

🔱 The Datta Avatar Trilogy

  • 1st: Sripada Srivallabha — Pithapuram/Kuruvapuram (1320–1350 CE)
  • 2nd: Narasimha Saraswati — Ganagapura/Srisailam (1378–1459 CE) prophesied by Sripada
  • 3rd: Swami Samarth of Akkalkot (~1800s) prophesied by Sripada

🌍 Cosmic Scope

Unique among sacred texts, the Charitamrutam reveals divine connections across all world religions: Jesus Christ as Dhruva reborn, Muhammad as a devotee of the formless Makkeshwar (Allah), and Adam & Eve (Adama & Havyavati) from the Bhavishya Purana — all flowing from one Parabrahman.

🛕 Primary Sacred Sites

  • Pithapuram — Maha Samsthanam (birthplace)
  • Kuruvapuram — Divine footprints, disappearance site
  • Bapatla — Sripada's childhood grace
  • Shankar Bhatt received the full story at Kuruvapuram, Pithapuram & Tripurantakam

Chapter Summaries — 51 Chapters
51 Adhyayas
Chapter 1
The Divine Command at Kanyakumari
Shankar Bhatt begins his pilgrimage. At Kanyakumari, Goddess Kanyaka Parameswari appears and commands: "Go to Kuruvapuram for the darshan of Sripada Srivallabha." He proceeds to Marutva Malai where the entire hill reverberates with "Sripada Rajam Saranam Prapadye!" The tiger at the cave is revealed as the transformed Brahmin Vyaghreswara Sarma.
Read Chapter 1 ↗
Chapter 2
Siddhendra Yogi and Siddha Yogindra
In the Kadamba forest, Siddhendra Yogi reveals the sacred history of Vasavi Kanyaka Parameswari and the Indra-consecrated Shiva linga. Near Madurai, a divine three-hooded serpent with diamond-studded hoods guides Shankar Bhatt to Siddha Yogindra's ashram.
Read Chapter 2 ↗
Chs. 3–4
Palaniswami — The 300-Year-Old Snake Siddha
In the hills near Chidambaram, Shankar Bhatt meets Palaniswami — a 300-year-old hermit who lives by inhabiting the corpse of a dead youth. Palaniswami revives the snake-bitten Madhava Namboodri using a live cobra as antidote, and reveals the four types of snakes and their cosmic significance.
Read Ch. 3 ↗
Chs. 3–4 cont.
Arrival at Kuruvapuram — Sripada's Footprints
PRIMARY DESTINATION. Shankar Bhatt reaches the sacred Krishna River island of Kuruvapuram. He receives darshan of Sripada's divine footprints and the vision of Vasavambika. Here the greatest cosmic revelations unfold: Jesus-Dhruva connection, Muhammad-Allah, Adama-Havyavati, and the vision of Sai Baba.
Read Ch. 4 ↗
Chapter 5
Tirupati, Kanipakam & the Karmic Gatekeeper
Shankar Bhatt visits Tirupati (Venkateswara = Sripada in his view) then reaches Kanipakam — the seat of the svayambhu Sri Varasiddhi Vinayaka. Here he meets Tirumala Das, a 70-year-old washerman-devotee at the temple. Tirumala Das reveals he was a learned Vedic scholar in his previous birth who was reborn as a washerman due to his miserly final thoughts at death. He now receives temple prasadam through trained dogs — a humble life transformed by devotion. PIVOTAL MOMENT: Tirumala Das commands Shankar Bhatt to begin writing the Charitamrutam and directs him to seek Vatsavayi Narasimha Varma at Pithapuram. He also narrates the miraculous origin of the Kanipakam idol: three quarrelling brothers healed when a svayambhu Vinayaka emerged from a well they were digging. Without this meeting, the entire Charitamrutam biography — and all the soul-prophecies it contains — might never have been recorded.
Read Chapter 5 ↗
Chs. 6–7
Pithapuram — Sripada's Birthplace and Family
Shankar Bhatt arrives at Pithapuram, the Maha Samsthanam. He learns of Sripada's family: grandfather Bapannavadhani (reincarnation of Nanda), grandmother Rajamamba, father Appalaraja Sarma (reincarnation of Vishnu Datta), mother Sumati Maharani (reincarnation of Somidevamma).
Read Ch. 6 ↗
Chs. 8–10
Vyaghreswara Sarma — The Tiger Liberation
Sripada directed the dunce Brahmin Vyaghreswara Sarma to Badrinath in a dream. He meditated on tigers, became a tiger, and Sripada walked on water to meet him at the Krishna River. Sripada rode him across the river; the tiger's skin became Sripada's seat. Final liberation attained.
Read Ch. 8 ↗
Chs. 9, 11, 41
Siblings — Healed and Prophesied
Srīdhararaja Sarma (born blind, healed; next life: Samardha Ramadas, guru of Shivaji) and Ramaraja Sarma (born lame, healed; next life: Avadhuta Sridhara / Sridhar Swami). Both are partial manifestations of Dattatreya. Vasavi Kanyaka — the cosmic sister who gave power enabling Mary's virgin birth.
Read Ch. 9 ↗
Chs. 12–15
Vatsavayi Narasimha Varma — Future Shivaji
Narasimha Varma, the Kshatriya nobleman and Sripada's closest devotee, is revealed to reincarnate as Shivaji Maharaja. Sripada affectionately called him "grandfather." He was present for the divine marriage proclamation of Sripada's parents.
Read Ch. 12 ↗
Chs. 13–16
Pynda Venkatappayya Sreshti — The Wealthy Devotee
The wealthy Vaishya merchant devotee of Pithapuram. Sripada gifted a house to the poor Appalaraja Sarma at his devotion. Sripada once teased him: "Grandfather, do we belong to the Markandeya lineage?" — hinting at their deep soul connection. Runs throughout the book.
Read Ch. 13 ↗
Chapter 15
The Arrogant Priest and the Maharaja
The priest of the Kukkuteswara Shiva temple and the local Maharaja refused to follow Sripada's commands. The Maharaja lost all his powers by the Lord's grace. The priest's future births were also prophesied.
Read Chapter 15 ↗
Chs. 17–19
Dhandiswami — The Mute Monk
Dhandiswami, an arrogant monk who challenged Sripada's divinity, was struck mute as a lesson in humility. The episode illustrates the fatal danger of approaching a divine avatar with ego and scepticism.
Read Ch. 17 ↗
Chs. 20–21
Raghavendra Swami — Prophesied Centuries Earlier
Sripada prophesied the coming of Raghavendra Swami (1595–1671 CE) — an incarnation of Prahlada — centuries before his birth. The shrine at Mantralayam on the Tungabhadra river was thus cosmically ordained by Sripada Himself.
Read Ch. 20 ↗
Chs. 22–24
Vallabha Das — The Cobbler-Healer
Vallabha Das, the cobbler devotee of Sripada, was given the power to heal all diseases. He became a renowned healer in Pithapuram, embodying Sripada's teaching that the Lord's grace transcends all caste distinctions.
Read Ch. 22 ↗
Chs. 25–26
Abbanna the Snake Charmer
Abbanna, the snake charmer, was given a sacred task by Sripada. The cosmic relationship between serpents and divinity — a recurring theme — is explored through Abbanna's devotion and service.
Read Ch. 25 ↗
Chs. 27–30
Vasavi Kanyaka — World Mother and Cosmic Sister
Vasavi Kanyaka Parameswari, identified as Sripada's cosmic sister, is an incarnation of the World Mother. On Sravana full moon she ties raksha bandhan to Sripada. Her divine power was given to the soul of Mary enabling Jesus Christ's virgin birth. The story of Brihat Sila Nagaram (now Penugonda) is revealed.
Read Ch. 27 ↗
Chs. 31–32
The Interfaith Cosmic Revelation
THE MOST REMARKABLE SECTION. Sripada reveals: (1) Jesus Christ = Dhruva reborn in Kali Yuga; called Narayana "Father." (2) Muhammad (Maha Mathi) = devotee of formless Makkeshwar (Allah). (3) Adama and Havyavati (Adam and Eve) in the "western island" — Kali entered as a serpent. All divine names flow from one Parabrahman.
Read Ch. 31 ↗
Chs. 33–35
Vedanta Sarma — The Soul-Splitting
The extraordinary episode where Vedanta Sarma's soul was simultaneously incarnated as himself, his three wives, cobbler Bangarayya, AND a cow. Next life: priests at Kuruvapuram. Illustrates Sripada's omnipotence over the cycles of karma and rebirth.
Read Ch. 33 ↗
Chs. 36–37
Dharma Gupta — The Long-Distance Devotee
Dharma Gupta became Shankar Bhatt's long-term travel companion after meeting him in the Pithapuram area. His story of encountering Sripada's grace and his steadfast devotion over long years of wandering is narrated at length.
Read Ch. 36 ↗
Chs. 38–39
Bangārappa — Black Magic Reversed
Bangārappa attempted to use black magic against a devotee. The magic fell back on him with full force, resulting in his suffering. A powerful warning about the impossibility of harming those under Sripada's protection.
Read Ch. 38 ↗
Chs. 40–46
Tripurantakam — Bhaskara Sastry's Revelation
THE CRUCIAL SECTION. Bhaskara Sastry, priest at Tripurantakam, gives Shankar Bhatt the fullest account of why Sripada left Pithapuram. He receives Sripada's copper sandals — a supreme blessing. All events after Sripada's disappearance from public view are narrated here.
Read Ch. 40 ↗
Chs. 46–48
Journey to Kashi and the North
Shankar Bhatt journeys to Kashi/Varanasi with the old sanyasi whom Sripada had directed there. Sripada had told him: "Go to Kashi, live there, leave your body there." The eternal city is described in its cosmic significance.
Read Ch. 46 ↗
Chs. 49–50
The Himalayas — Badrinath and Kedarnath
The pilgrimage reaches the Himalayas. Badrinath (where Vyaghreswara Sarma had earlier meditated, meeting Babaji's lineage) and Kedarnath — Lord Shiva's Jyotirlinga. The cosmic journey reaches its northern culmination.
Read Ch. 49 ↗
Chapter 51
Conclusion — The Eternal Presence
Shankar Bhatt concludes the narrative. Sripada declares His eternal presence: "I am always at Kuruvapuram. Those who come with faith will never leave empty-handed." The prophecy of Narasimha Saraswati and Swami Samarth as future avatars is confirmed. Sripada Rajam Saranam Prapadye.
Read Chapter 51 ↗

Key Teachings — Sripada Srivallabha Charitamrutam
🕉️ The Primary Mantra
Composed by Sripada Srivallabha Himself
Sripada Rajam Saranam Prapadye
Sripada Rajam Saranam Prapadye
Sripada Rajam Saranam Prapadye
Meaning: "I surrender to the King Sripada as my refuge." This mantra resonates throughout the Charitamrutam — the entire hill of Marutva Malai reverberates with it; siddhas chant it; the tiger at the cave chants it. Sripada instructed Shankar Bhatt that chanting this mantra with sincere devotion is sufficient for liberation. The mantra also invokes the Datta parampara — "Sripada Rajam" acknowledges His kingship over the spiritual realm, while "Saranam Prapadye" (I take refuge) is the gesture of complete surrender that all Guru devotees are asked to make.
🔱 Dattatreya Dhyana Shloka
Meditation verse on the Trimurthi avatar
Dattatreya Hare Krishna
Unmatta Ananda Dayaka
Digambara Muni Sreshtha
Dushtasangha Vinashaka
Meaning: "Dattatreya, hail! Joy-giver beyond measure. Naked ascetic and best of sages, destroyer of evil company." Dattatreya is born of Atri and Anasuya — the perfect embodiment of Brahma-Vishnu-Shiva. The Charitamrutam describes how He roams the earth as an avadhuta (liberated wanderer), sometimes appearing drunk, naked, accompanied by dogs — testing devotees' inner vision. Those who see only the outer form miss Him; those who see the divine in all forms receive His grace.
🌟 Core Teaching: Universal Oneness
The cosmic theology of the Charitamrutam
The Charitamrutam makes a radical declaration: all divine names across all traditions point to one Parabrahman. • Makkeshwar (Lord of Mecca) = the same who is called Allah — formless, infinite • Jesus = Dhruva, reborn in a Western island; Mary received Vasavi Kanyaka's power • Adama and Havyavati (Adam and Eve) are in the Bhavishya Purana • Sripada personally visited Hanuman in Nepal and asked him to incarnate as Sai Baba and "worship God as Allah" Teaching: There is no barrier between traditions in the vision of a true Guru.
📿 The Three Dattatreya Avatars
As prophesied by Sripada Srivallabha Himself
Sripada declared the three Kali Yuga avataras of Dattatreya: 1. Sripada Srivallabha — born Pithapuram (1320s CE), disappeared Kuruvapuram (1350 CE) 2. Narasimha Saraswati — born Lad Karanja (circa 1378 CE), seat at Ganagapura, departed Kardali Vana (1459 CE) 3. Swami Samarth — seat at Akkalkot (~1800–1878 CE) Sripada also indicated that Shirdi Sai Baba would carry the divine light forward — the fourth in this cosmic chain. The Charitamrutam itself contains a vision of Sai Baba at Kuruvapuram — centuries before Sai Baba was born.
📖 Navavidha Bhakti (Nine Forms of Devotion)
As taught in the Charitamrutam
1. Sravanam — hearing sacred stories (katha) 2. Kirtanam — singing praises (bhajan) 3. Smaranam — remembering, meditating on the divine 4. Pada Sevanam — serving the Guru's feet 5. Archanam — ritual worship, offering 6. Vandanam — prostration, humility 7. Dasyam — servitude, surrender 8. Sakhyam — friendship with the divine 9. Atma Nivedanam — complete self-surrender The Charitamrutam teaches that devotion to the Guru is the supreme path — He contains all nine within Himself.
⚡ The Guru Tattva Teaching
On the nature of the Sadguru
"The Guru is not a person — He is a principle (tattva). The Sadguru is the living embodiment of Parabrahman. His grace descends regardless of time and space — a sincere devotee separated by centuries can still receive His direct blessing." The Charitamrutam itself is considered a living Guru — reading it with devotion and surrender is equivalent to receiving direct initiation from Sripada. His grace is not bound by His physical presence at Kuruvapuram in 1350 CE.
🏛️ Historical Context — 14th Century Deccan
The world in which Sripada appeared
Sripada's era (circa 1320–1350 CE): • The Vijayanagara Empire is newly founded (1336 CE) — the great Hindu bastion of South India • The Delhi Sultanate controls North India under the Tughlaq dynasty • The Bahmani Sultanate is rising in the Deccan • Kabir (1440–1518) will come later; Mirabai (1498–1547) even later • Namdev (1270–1350) is Sripada's approximate contemporary Sripada's cosmic revelations — unifying Hindu, Islamic, and even Abrahamic traditions — were centuries ahead of their time. In an era of religious conflict, He declared the unity of all divine names.
Stories & Leelas — Charitamrutam
33 Leelas
🐍
The Cobra Revives the Dead Man
MiracleChapter 3–4Shankar Bhatt's First Vision
At Chidambaram, the 300-year-old Siddha Palaniswami calls a live king-cobra from the forest. A man bitten by a snake lies dead. Palaniswami places the cobra on the corpse — it sucks out the venom and the dead man breathes again. This is the first miracle Shankar Bhatt witnesses on his pilgrimage. It plants the central question of the entire book: who is this Sripada whose name even Palaniswami speaks with awe?
🐯
The Brahmin Who Became Sripada's Tiger
MiracleChapter 20–21Karmic Reversal
Vyaghreswara Sarma — a Brahmin who had lived in the form of a tiger due to his past karma — recognises Sripada as the Divine. He prostrates himself before Sripada and carries Sripada across the Krishna river on his back. Sripada then liberates him from the tiger-birth. The Charitamrutam uses this episode to explain how karma from past lives manifests as the body one inhabits — and how the Guru's touch dissolves it instantly.
🌊
Sripada Walks on the Krishna River
MiracleMultiple ChaptersDivine Power
On multiple occasions, Sripada walks upon the surface of the Krishna river as if it were solid ground. Witnesses on the banks see the young Brahmin boy moving effortlessly across the water. This miracle — repeated at different locations — establishes Sripada's mastery over the physical elements. The river itself, as the Dakshini Ganga (Krishnaveni), recognises and honours its Lord.
🙏
Sripada Visits Hanuman in Nepal — Sai is Born
Cosmic RevelationChapter 1 & 3Most Astonishing Episode
Sripada personally travels to Nepal and meets Hanuman — the eternal, deathless devotee of Rama. He instructs Hanuman to incarnate at Shirdi and to worship God as Allah. This is the Charitamrutam's most staggering revelation: Shirdi Sai Baba is Hanuman incarnate — sent personally by Sripada (the first Datta Avatar) to establish the bridge between Sanatana Dharma and Islam in the 19th century. The entire Datta chain — Sripada, Narasimha Saraswati, Swami Samarth, Sai Baba — is thus a planned cosmic mission.
🌿
The Leper's Complete Healing
MiracleMiddle ChaptersTotal Surrender
A Brahmin suffering from advanced leprosy is brought to Sripada in a condition of total physical degradation. He does not ask for healing — only for the sight of Sripada. Sripada gives him sacred ash (vibhuti) and instructs him to apply it daily. Within days the leprosy vanishes completely, leaving clear skin. The episode's teaching: it was not the ash — it was the absence of personal desire in the devotee's heart that opened the channel for Sripada's healing grace.
🕉️
Sripada Directs the Old Monk to Kashi
Divine GraceMiddle ChaptersLiberation
An old sanyasi who had spent decades in spiritual practice approaches Sripada. Sripada gives him a precise instruction: "Go to Kashi immediately. Live there. Leave your body there." The monk obeys. Sripada had read the precise geography of his soul's liberation — Kashi (Varanasi) is the city where dying grants moksha automatically through Shiva's kashi-vasa-tarak-mantra. This episode establishes Sripada as a guide who coordinates not just life — but the perfect moment and location of death.
👶
Chintamani's Dead Child Restored to Life
MiracleChapter 28–30Maternal Faith
Chintamani's young child dies suddenly. Rather than weeping or arguing, she wraps the body, places it at Sripada's feet, and simply waits — without a word. No demand, no bargaining with God. Only total surrender. Sripada is moved: "This woman has not placed conditions on her bhakti." He restores the child to life. The Charitamrutam presents this as the definitive model of Nishkama Bhakti: faith that places no conditions on the Lord.
🔥
Vasavi Kanyaka — The World Mother's Fire
Cosmic EpisodeChapter 27–32Most Extensive Episode
The Charitamrutam's longest and most cosmologically rich episode. Vasavi Kanyaka Parameswari — identified as Sripada's cosmic sister, the World Mother of the Komati (Vysya) community — chose self-immolation with 16,000 maidens rather than submit to Virabhadra's dishonourable demand. Her sacrifice ignited a divine fire that burned for years. She had given her divine power to the soul of Mary (Miriam) before she ascended — enabling the virgin birth of Jesus. Shankar Bhatt receives this entire revelation from Siddhendra Yogi in the Kadamba forest.
✝️
Jesus Christ — Dhruva Reborn
Cosmic RevelationChapter 29–31Inter-faith
The Charitamrutam makes the astonishing declaration: Jesus Christ (Yesu Christu) is Dhruva — the great Vishnu devotee of the Bhagavata Purana — reborn in a human body in Judea. His divine power was seeded by Vasavi Kanyaka who gave her shakti to Mary's soul. His crucifixion had a cosmic purpose in Parabrahman's plan. The Charitamrutam is unique among Hindu sacred texts for integrating the Christian revelation into the Sanatana framework without subordinating it.
☪️
Muhammad and the Formless Makkeshwar
Cosmic RevelationChapter 29–31Inter-faith
The Charitamrutam reveals that the divine reality Muhammad encountered — Allah — is Makkeshwar, the formless aspect of Shiva worshipped by a yogi named Mecca Sadhu in a previous cosmic cycle. Muhammad is described as a sincere devotee of this formless divine presence. The Charitamrutam integrates Islam not as an opposing religion but as one stream of the same Parabrahman's revelation flowing through different lands and peoples.
🔮
Sripada Prophesies the Three Future Avatars
ProphecyFinal ChaptersParampara
In the closing chapters, Sripada gives Shankar Bhatt detailed prophecies: "After me, Dattatreya will appear as Narasimha Saraswati — born in Lad Karanja, will establish the seat at Ganagapura." Then: "After Narasimha Saraswati, the Avatar will appear at Prajnapura (Akkalkot) as Swami Samarth." These fulfilled prophecies — recorded before Narasimha Saraswati's birth — are the Charitamrutam's most powerful internal verification. Modern readers add: Sai Baba as the fourth fulfilment.
🌟
The Final Disappearance at Kuruvapuram
Divine ExitChapter 51Mahanirvana
At age 30, in 1350 CE, Sripada Srivallabha announces His departure. He walks to Kuruvapuram — the small island in the Krishna River — and simply disappears into the brahmanda (cosmic egg / absolute reality). No body. No death. Only a pair of divine footprints (padukas) remain on the Krishna riverbank, still worshipped today. The Charitamrutam ends with Shankar Bhatt receiving the command: "Write everything you have witnessed. This book will be My living presence for future devotees."
🤔
Shankar Bhatt's Doubts — Each Test Deepens Faith
Narrator's JourneyThroughout all 51 Ch.Faith Tested
The Charitamrutam is unique: its narrator Shankar Bhatt is not a perfect devotee — he doubts, questions, and tests repeatedly. Each time he doubts Sripada's divinity, Sripada responds not with anger but with another miracle or revelation that dissolves the specific doubt. The book is structured as a progressive destruction of Shankar Bhatt's intellectual resistance: each chapter peels away one more layer of scepticism. By the final chapter, Shankar Bhatt does not believe in Sripada — he knows. The Charitamrutam uses this narrative device to invite the reader into the same journey: you are Shankar Bhatt. Your doubts are welcome. The book will answer them all.
🧅
Parable of the Five Sheaths — What You Are Not
ParableChapter 18 — Vedantic TeachingJnana Teaching
Sripada explains the Pancha Kosha (five sheaths) through the parable of a lamp inside five nested boxes: the innermost box holds the lamp (the Self/Atman); around it are four increasingly opaque boxes — bliss body, wisdom body, mind body, prana body, and outermost the food body (the physical form). Most people believe they ARE the outermost box. The spiritual journey is simply opening box after box, recognising "not this, not this" (Neti, Neti), until the lamp is reached. "You cannot become the lamp," Sripada told Shankar Bhatt. "You already are. You just need to open the boxes."
🏺
Parable of the Pot and the Sky
ParableChapter 20Advaita Teaching
Sripada holds up a clay pot and asks Shankar Bhatt: "The sky inside this pot — is it different from the sky outside?" Shankar Bhatt sees immediately: the pot's clay creates the apparent boundary, but the sky-space inside and outside is the same continuous sky. "Break the pot," says Sripada. "Where did the inside sky go?" It merged with the outside. "This is birth, life, and moksha," Sripada explains. "The clay (body) creates the apparent boundary. The Atman inside is the same as Brahman outside. Death breaks the pot. Liberation is knowing — before death — that the sky was never separate." The Charitamrutam presents this as one of Sripada's core philosophical parables.
🌿
The 500-Year-Old Siddhendra Yogi Reveals Hidden History
RevelationChapter 2Hidden Cosmic History
In the Kadamba forest near Marutva Malai, Shankar Bhatt encounters Siddhendra Yogi — a siddha who has lived for over 500 years through yogic mastery. This ancient master reveals to Shankar Bhatt the hidden cosmic history that the Charitamrutam will then unfold chapter by chapter: Vasavi Kanyaka's sacrifice, the connection to Mary and Jesus, Muhammad and Makkeshwar, the Atmalinga at Gokarna, and Sripada's true identity as Dattatreya incarnate. Siddhendra Yogi functions as the Charitamrutam's first great teacher — preparing Shankar Bhatt's consciousness to receive what Sripada Himself will later reveal.
🌳
Adam and Eve — Adama and Havyavati of the Bhavishya Purana
Cosmic RevelationChapter 29–31Biblical Connection
The Charitamrutam makes the extraordinary identification: the biblical Adam and Eve are "Adama and Havyavati" mentioned in the Bhavishya Purana — the Purana of prophecies. The garden of Eden corresponds to a cosmic garden described in Vedic cosmology. Their "fall" corresponds to the descent of consciousness into duality — the same as the Hindu concept of Maya's veil descending. The Charitamrutam presents this not as Hindu appropriation of Christianity but as the same event described in two different civilisational languages — both true, both incomplete without each other.
🛕
The Atmalinga of Gokarna — Ravana's Sacred Error
Cosmic EpisodeReferenced in Chapter 28Ramayana Connection
The Charitamrutam references the story of the Atmalinga — the Self-Linga of Shiva which Ravana obtained from Kailash through extraordinary tapas. Shiva gave it on condition: Ravana must not set it down before reaching Lanka. Ganesha (disguised as a boy) tricked Ravana into setting it down at Gokarna — where it became permanently fixed. The Charitamrutam uses this episode to explain why Gokarna is one of the most powerful Shiva shrines: it holds the Atmalinga intended for Lanka — the most powerful Linga Shiva ever gave to a mortal. Sripada visited Gokarna during His pilgrimage. The cosmic geography of the Charitamrutam connects Pithapuram, Gokarna, and Kuruvapuram as three points of a divine triangle.
👨‍👦
Sripada Revives His Own Father
MiracleChapter 8Family Episode
When Sripada's father Appalaraja Sarma died of sudden illness, the household was plunged into grief. The young Sripada — only a child — walked calmly to his father's body, placed both hands on the chest, and sat in silence for a few minutes. His father's breathing resumed. He sat up. When asked by family members how this happened, Sripada said only: "Father wasn't done yet. He had more dharma to fulfil." The episode establishes that Sripada's miraculous power was not something He acquired through tapas or initiation — it was inherent from birth. He was born fully realised.
👑
The Vatsavayi Chieftain's Devotion
Historical EpisodeChapter 6–7Royal Devotion
The ruling Vatsavayi family of Pithapuram were among the earliest and most fervent devotees of Sripada. The chieftain Narasimha Varma himself received Sripada's direct blessing — and his subsequent prosperity was attributed to Sripada's grace. The Charitamrutam records several episodes where the chieftain's court witnessed miracles: Sripada appearing in two places simultaneously, knowing events happening in distant villages, and predicting the outcomes of the chieftain's administrative decisions with perfect accuracy. The royal patronage gave Sripada's presence at Pithapuram official legitimacy — the king himself bowed.
🌊
The Descent of the Godavari — Dakshina Ganga
River OriginChapter 2 & 48Sacred Geography
The Charitamrutam explains the Godavari's celestial origin: Brahma requested Vishnu that the purifying energy of the Ganga flow through the Deccan for the benefit of souls who could not reach the northern Ganga. Vishnu sent this energy southward; it emerged as the Godavari at Brahmagiri (Tryambakeshvara), where Shiva's Jyotirlinga marks the spot. Called "Dakshina Ganga" (the Southern Ganga), the Godavari carries the same liberation-granting power. Shankar Bhatt's pilgrimage begins near this sacred arc — the entire narrative of the Charitamrutam unfolds within the sacred zone between the Godavari (north) and the Krishna (south).
🌊
The Sacred Origin of the Krishna (Krishnaveni)
River OriginMultiple ChaptersSacred Geography
The Charitamrutam refers to the Krishna river as "Krishnaveni" — containing the essence of Lord Krishna Himself. The river rises at Mahabaleshwar in the Western Ghats and flows east through Karnataka and Andhra. The Charitamrutam explains that the Krishna river was consecrated when Lord Krishna's divine energy was infused into it at the moment of His cosmic dissolution — making it one of the most spiritually potent rivers of the Deccan. Sripada's chosen seat at Kuruvapuram — a river island — is not accidental: the Krishna itself is His throne.
🏞️
The Tungabhadra — River of the Southern Ramayana
River OriginReferenced in pilgrimage arcSacred Geography
The Tungabhadra — which joins the Krishna — is the river at whose banks Kishkindha (the monkey kingdom of the Ramayana) stood. Hanuman's homeland. The Charitamrutam's revelation that Sripada personally visited Hanuman in Nepal links the two rivers cosmically: Hanuman's Kishkindha sat on the Tungabhadra; Sripada's Kuruvapuram sits on the Krishna. When these two rivers merge, so do the two divine streams — Ramayana and the Datta Parampara — flowing into one sacred geography.
🔥
Vasavi Kanyaka's Fire Sacrifice — The Vow That Shaped Pithapuram
Historical SacrificeChapter 4–5 — Pre-history of PithapuramCosmic Vow
Centuries before Sripada's birth, the merchant community of Pithapuram was oppressed by Virabhadra, a Kshatriya chieftain who demanded the most beautiful girl of the community — Vasavi Kanyaka — as his wife. Vasavi Kanyaka refused: she had vowed celibacy and consecrated her life to God. When the chieftain threatened to destroy the entire community if she refused, Vasavi Kanyaka made a cosmic vow: "If I cannot live in honour, I will not live." She led 16 young virgins of her community in a fire sacrifice — entering the flames rather than submitting to dishonour. The Charitamrutam reveals that this act of supreme dharmic sacrifice created a permanent spiritual vortex at Pithapuram. It was specifically this accumulated sacrificial energy that made Pithapuram the right birthplace for Dattatreya's first modern incarnation. Sripada is born into the fire Vasavi created.
👩
Sumathi's Prayer — The Mother Who Asked for God as Her Son
Divine Birth StoryChapter 1 — Before the BirthMother's Piety
Sumathi Maharani (Sripada's mother) was no ordinary devotee. The Charitamrutam records that she performed intense tapas for years, praying not for wealth, health, or children in the ordinary sense — but that the Lord Himself would take birth through her. Her prayer was precise and extraordinary: "I want You as my son." The cosmos responded. At the moment of Sripada's birth, the house filled with fragrance, music was heard without musicians, and the newborn sat in padmasana before the astonished midwife. Sumathi knew instantly: her son was not her son. He had arrived on loan. This knowing shaped her entire relationship with Sripada — she served Him as a devotee, not only as a mother.
🧘
Sripada Takes Sannyasa — Against All Family Wishes
RenunciationChapter 9The Great Departure
At a young age — against the vigorous protests of His family, who had already arranged His marriage — Sripada took sannyasa (formal renunciation). He did not ask permission. He did not negotiate. He calmly placed the sacred thread down, spoke His truth to His parents, and walked out. His mother wept. His father argued. But Sripada had already seen through the illusion of family bonds as an ordinary son — He was Dattatreya. He could not play the role for long. The Charitamrutam presents this departure not as cruelty but as the highest gift: His presence in the world as a free avadhuta would eventually liberate hundreds of thousands. Keeping Him in a household would have served one family. Freeing Him served all.
👦
The Brahmin of Bapatla — Three Generations of Karma Cleared
MiracleChapter 23–24Karmic Purification
A Brahmin from Bapatla brought his dead son to Sripada at Kuruvapuram. But Sripada did something unexpected: before reviving the boy, He sat with the father and revealed the karmic history of his family across three generations — specific sins, specific consequences, specific accumulated burdens. The father wept in recognition. Then Sripada said: "The debt is now seen. What is seen is already half dissolved." He touched the boy. The boy breathed. Sripada's teaching embedded in the miracle: He revives, but also uses the moment of extreme openness — a father's terror — to deliver the deepest possible teaching about karma. The crisis is the classroom.
🔱
Sripada and the Nath Tradition — The Hidden Thread
Cosmic LineageChapter 30–32Nath Connection
The Charitamrutam reveals that the Nath Sampradaya — the tradition of Macchindranath, Gorakshanath, and the great siddhas of medieval India — is not separate from the Datta Parampara. It is the same river seen from the northern bank. Dattatreya initiated both streams. When Shankar Bhatt asks why the Nath tradition doesn't mention Dattatreya explicitly, Sripada smiles and says: "The river doesn't mention the mountain it descended from. It simply flows." The great Nath siddhas were Datta devotees in essence even when they used different names and forms. The Charitamrutam locates Sripada at the junction of these streams — not as founder of one but as the source of all.
🌌
The 72 Crore Yojana Teaching — Vedic Cosmology Explained
Cosmic TeachingChapter 35Vedic Astronomy
In one of the Charitamrutam's most remarkable episodes, Sripada explains the Vedic model of the cosmos to Shankar Bhatt — not as mythology but as precise spiritual geography. He describes the 14 lokas (worlds), the measurement system in yojanas, the placement of the Saptarishis (seven great sages) in the stellar realm, and how the liberated souls travel through the cosmic grid. What astonishes Shankar Bhatt is not the content — it is that Sripada describes each realm as somewhere He has been, as a traveller describes cities He has visited. He is not reciting scripture. He is giving a travelogue.
🌺
Meenakshi — The Devotee Who Served in Silence
DevotionChapter 11Silent Service
Among Sripada's early devotees in Pithapuram was Meenakshi — a woman of modest means who cooked for Sripada daily, without fanfare or expectation. The Charitamrutam records that Meenakshi never asked for a single miracle or blessing. She just cooked and served. One day Sripada called her into His presence and said: "You think you are serving me. But look." He opened her awareness: she saw her entire family's accumulated karma dissolving in the act of cooking for the Lord. Her husband's chronic illness lifted that week. Her son's failing prospects reversed within a month. She had been receiving miracles continuously — she just hadn't noticed, because she wasn't looking for them.
🙏
Sudhindra Tirtha Recognises Sripada's Divinity
Great RecognitionChapter 14Dvaita Tradition
Sudhindra Tirtha, the pontiff of the Uttaradi Math (the Dvaita Vedanta institution in the Madhva parampara), came to Pithapuram during his pilgrimage circuit. As a senior pontiff, he was accustomed to receiving reverence — not giving it. When he encountered the young Sripada, something extraordinary happened: Sudhindra Tirtha spontaneously prostrated. His astonished disciples asked why. The great pontiff said simply: "Because He is what all the Upanishads describe. I have been teaching that reality all my life. Today I saw it." He became a devotee. The Charitamrutam uses this episode to establish: Sripada was recognised not just by simple villagers or desperate supplicants — but by the greatest scholars of the age. Separately, in Chapters 20–21, Sripada prophesied that a great saint initiated in this same Uttaradi Math lineage would establish his brindavana at Mantralayam — a prophecy fulfilled by Raghavendra Swami 330 years later.
🌱
Parable of the Seed and the Fruit — On Karma's Timing
ParableChapter 26Karma Teaching
A devotee asks Sripada: "Why do bad people prosper and good people suffer? Where is the justice?" Sripada points to a mango tree in fruit: "When was this seed planted?" The devotee calculates — perhaps twenty years ago. "So the man who planted the seed is not necessarily the man eating the fruit," Sripada says. "And the man eating the fruit may not remember planting the seed." Karma's accounting spans lifetimes, not years. The good man suffering today planted differently in another life. The bad man prospering today will account for this life's seeds — in another season, in another body. "But," Sripada adds, "the man who comes to the Guru skips the queue. The Guru burns the seeds before they can fruit."
🌊
Parable of the River and the Ocean — On Moksha
ParableChapter 44Liberation Teaching
A scholar asks Sripada: "When I attain moksha, do I lose myself? I am afraid of dissolution." Sripada asks: "Is the river afraid to reach the ocean?" The scholar thinks. "It loses its banks," he says. "It loses its name," Sripada agrees. "But does it lose its water?" No. The water remains — it simply becomes the ocean. "You will not lose your awareness in moksha," Sripada explains. "You will gain every other awareness. You were a cup of ocean worrying about losing yourself. The ocean doesn't miss the cup. It celebrates the water returning home." The scholar wept with relief.
About Shri Gurucharitra

🕉️ The Divine Subject

Narasimha Saraswati — the second Dattatreya avatar in Kali Yuga. Born circa 1378 CE in Lad Karanja (Karanjnagari), Maharashtra. His primary seat was Ganagapura (confluence of Bhima and Amaraja rivers, Karnataka). He departed from Kardali Vana near Srisailam in 1459 CE.

✍️ The Author

Saraswati Gangadhara — a direct disciple of Narasimha Saraswati. The book is composed in Marathi Ovibaddha (verse form) as a dialogue between Siddhamuni (the author's disciple) and Namdharak (a devoted seeker). Written c.15th–16th century CE.

📚 The Book

  • 52 chapters (Adhyayas)
  • Marathi language (Ovibaddha verse)
  • Covers both Sripada Srivallabha AND Narasimha Saraswati
  • One of the most sacred texts of the Datta tradition
  • Reading the entire Gurucharitra is considered a 7-day parayan
  • Edition referenced: Marathi Ovibaddha original (c.15th–16th century CE). English chapter translations sourced from mantrastotrajyoti.blogspot.com; full text at omnisai.org/gurucharitra

🔱 Prophesied by Sripada

In the Charitamrutam, Sripada Srivallabha directly prophesied the coming of Narasimha Saraswati as the second Dattatreya avatar. Sripada's ashes spiritually fell at Ganagapura, consecrating it centuries before Narasimha Saraswati made it His seat.

🛕 Primary Sacred Sites

  • Ganagapura / Gangapur — main 20-year seat (Karnataka)
  • Narsoba Wadi (Narsobachi Wadi) — confluence of Krishna & Panchaganga (Maharashtra)
  • Audumbar — sacred fig tree grove on the Krishna
  • Kardali Vana — final disappearance near Srisailam
  • Kashi — where He took sanyas diksha

📿 Significance

The Gurucharitra is not merely a biography — it is a comprehensive manual of devotional life (dharma, parayan, fasting, service to Guru) whose regular reading is said to remove all afflictions. Reading it on certain tithis (lunar days) grants specific blessings according to tradition.

📖 Read Full Text Online →
Chapter Summaries — Shri Gurucharitra
52 Adhyayas
Adhyaya 1 ✦
Namdharak Sees Shri Guru in Dream
Namdharak, a devout seeker, sees Narasimha Saraswati in a vivid dream and sets out in search of His living presence. Near Gangapur he meets the Siddha Muni who assures him that the Guru's grace is ever-present and begins to narrate the sacred story.
Read Adhyaya 1 ↗
Adhyaya 2 ✦
Siddha Muni Guides Namdharak
Siddha Muni instructs Namdharak on the supreme importance of Guru bhakti — explaining that the Trimurti (Brahma, Vishnu, Shiva) are one in the form of Shri Guru Dattatreya. He reveals the sacred Gurucharitra text and its power to grant all four goals of human life.
Read Adhyaya 2 ↗
Adhyaya 3 ✦
Durwas Curses King Ambarish
The story of King Ambarish and sage Durwas — a tale of Ekadashi vrata, the king's unwavering devotion to Vishnu, and Durwas's curse. Vishnu protects His devotee while Durwas must atone. This establishes why Dattatreya (Vishnu's portion) incarnates — to protect and guide devoted souls.
Read Adhyaya 3 ↗
Adhyaya 4 ✦
Birth of Shri Dattatreya
The Trimurti disguise themselves as wandering mendicants and test the pativrata (chastity) of Anasuya, wife of Rishi Atri. Her devotion transforms all three into infants nursing at her breast. Atri returns and they reveal themselves; Brahma becomes Chandra, Shiva becomes Durvas, and Vishnu remains as Dattatreya — the Adi Guru.
Read Adhyaya 4 ↗
Adhyaya 5 ✦
Birth of Shripad Shri Vallabha
Shri Datta appears to the devoted Sumatha at Peethapur and blesses her. Her son born of this divine grace is called Shripad (after Datta's name) — Shripad Shri Vallabha, the first Dattatreya avatar of Kali Yuga. His thread ceremony at age 7 reveals his extraordinary Vedic knowledge.
Read Adhyaya 5 ↗
Adhyaya 6 ✦
Ravana and Gokarna Mahabaleshwar
The story of Ravana obtaining the Atma-Linga from Shiva for his mother, and Ganesh (disguised as a Brahmin boy) tricking Ravana into placing the Linga on the ground at Gokarna. The Linga becomes immovable — Mahabaleshwar — explaining why Shripad Shri Vallabha stayed at Gokarna for three years.
Read Adhyaya 6 ↗
Adhyaya 7 ✦
Soumini and Madayanti at Gokarna
King Kalmashpad is cursed by Vasishtha to become a Brahmaraksha (demon). His devoted queen Madayanti prevents him from cursing Vasishtha in return. The king's redemption after visiting Gokarna and later encounters with Goutam Muni illustrate the cleansing power of this holy Jyotirlinga kshetra.
Read Adhyaya 7 ↗
Adhyaya 8 ✦
Shripad Shri Vallabha Blesses a Brahmani and Her Son
At Kuravapur on the Krishna river, a widowed Brahmin woman and her foolish son decide to end their lives in despair. Shripad Shri Vallabha appears on the riverbank and transforms the dull boy into a learned scholar with a single glance of grace, blessing the mother with long life and prosperity.
Read Adhyaya 8 ↗
Adhyaya 9
Shripad's Miracles at Kuravapur — Further Grace
Shripad Shri Vallabha continues to reside at Kuravapur island on the Krishna river, performing miracles for devoted pilgrims. The blind receive sight, the sick are healed, and devotees from distant lands arrive drawn by His divine fame. The significance of Kuravapur as a Dattatreya kshetra is established.
Read Adhyaya 9 ↗
Adhyaya 10
Shripad Shri Vallabha — Departure from Kuravapur
Shripad Shri Vallabha announces His departure from Kuravapur to undertake further pilgrimage. He instructs devotees to worship His sacred padukas (sandals) as His living presence at Kuravapur. He disappears into the river — fulfilling His prophecy that His next advent will be as Narasimha Saraswati.
Read Adhyaya 10 ↗
Adhyaya 11
Birth of Narasimha Saraswati at Karanjnagari
Narasimha Saraswati is born in Lad Karanja (Karanjnagari), Maharashtra, to Madhava Sharma and his devoted wife Amba. The infant utters the Vedic pranava mantra (Om) from birth. Brahmins declare him an avatar. The cosmic chain: Sripada prophesied this birth, and that prophecy is now fulfilled.
Read Adhyaya 11 ↗
Adhyaya 12
The Silent Child — Five Years Without Food
The young Narasimha Saraswati refuses food and remains silent for five years, absorbed in internal contemplation. His mother Amba is distressed. Sages advise that the child is divine and will speak when He receives Gayatri upadesh. The power of this pre-birth divine state is illustrated.
Read Adhyaya 12 ↗
Adhyaya 13
The Thread Ceremony — Upanayana and First Words
At His Upanayana (sacred thread ceremony), Narasimha Saraswati first opens His mouth to speak — and utters perfect Vedic chants, stunning all present. He performs Gayatri japa with such power that the priests bow to the child. His father Madhava Sharma passes away soon after, deepening His detachment.
Read Adhyaya 13 ↗
Adhyaya 14
Narasimha Saraswati Leaves Home
The young Narasimha Saraswati resolves to renounce home and undertake pilgrimage. Despite His mother Amba's grief, He blesses her and departs. He promises: "I am always with you. The Guru never abandons those who surrender." He sets out northward toward the sacred city of Kashi (Varanasi).
Read Adhyaya 14 ↗
Adhyaya 15
Journey to Kashi — Sanyas Diksha
Narasimha Saraswati arrives at Kashi (Varanasi) — the eternal city of Shiva. He receives sanyas diksha (renunciation initiation) from the great sanyasi Krishnananda Saraswati. He is given the sanyas name Narasimha Saraswati (Narasimha = "man-lion," the fierce form of Vishnu; Saraswati = the monastic lineage).
Read Adhyaya 15 ↗
Adhyaya 16
Narasimha Saraswati — Full Manifestation at Kashi
At Kashi, Narasimha Saraswati's divine powers manifest fully. He gives profound Vedic teachings to great scholars who come to test Him. He visits all the sacred sites of Kashi — Vishwanath, Annapoorna, Manikarnika Ghat — and undertakes austerities at the Panchaganga Ghat.
Read Adhyaya 16 ↗
Adhyaya 17
Journey South — Toward the Sangam Kshetras
Narasimha Saraswati leaves Kashi and travels south through pilgrimage sites — Prayag (Allahabad), Nasik, and toward the Deccan region. At each place He performs miracles and blesses devotees. The great rivers of the Deccan — Krishna, Bhima, Panchaganga — draw Him southward.
Read Adhyaya 17 ↗
Adhyaya 18
At Narsoba Wadi — The Sacred Sangam
Narasimha Saraswati arrives at Narsobachi Wadi (Narsoba Wadi) at the confluence of the Krishna and Panchaganga rivers. This becomes one of His principal seats. He reveals: "Dattatreya is always present here." The sacred Ashvattha (peepal) tree is consecrated as a living altar for Datta worship.
Read Adhyaya 18 ↗
Adhyaya 19
The Audumbar Tree — Dattatreya's Cosmic Abode
Narasimha Saraswati reveals the cosmic significance of the Audumbar tree (cluster fig, Ficus racemosa). He declares it one of Dattatreya's primary abodes in the physical world, prescribing circumambulation and worship. Several devotees receive liberation and healing under the Audumbar.
Read Adhyaya 19 ↗
Adhyaya 20
The Brahmin's Lame Wife — Grace of the Padukas
A devoted Brahmin's wife has been lame from birth. After serving the Guru's padukas with absolute devotion for years, Narasimha Saraswati appears to her in a dream and blesses her. She wakes to find herself completely healed. The power of paduka seva (service to the Guru's sandals) is established.
Read Adhyaya 20 ↗
Adhyaya 21
Brahmin Ambarisha — Dead Son Raised to Life
THE MOST CELEBRATED MIRACLE. Brahmin Ambarisha's son dies of a snakebite. The distraught mother carries the dead child to Narasimha Saraswati and lays him at His feet, declaring she will not leave without her living son. Moved by her faith, the Guru brings the boy back to life: "It is your faith that did this."
Read Adhyaya 21 ↗
Adhyaya 22
The Leper Brahmin — Healed by Surrender
A Brahmin afflicted with severe leprosy approaches Narasimha Saraswati after all medical and religious remedies have failed. He surrenders completely — body, mind, and pride. Directed to bathe in the sacred river at the Guru's command, he emerges with clean, luminous skin. Full surrender is the only medicine needed.
Read Adhyaya 22 ↗
Adhyaya 23
The Childless Couple — Grant of a Son
A Brahmin couple childless after many years of marriage approaches Narasimha Saraswati with deep grief. The Guru blesses them, prescribing specific worship and service. Within a year they receive a son, who grows to be a great scholar. The child is said to be the Guru's own gift — a Guru-prasad putra.
Read Adhyaya 23 ↗
Adhyaya 24
Trivikrama Bharati — The Scholar Surrenders
Trivikrama Bharati, the most renowned Vedic scholar of the region, comes to debate Narasimha Saraswati, confident in his mastery. When the Guru reveals cosmic truths and interprets scriptures in ways that transcend all learning, Trivikrama Bharati falls at His feet, weeping, and becomes His closest disciple.
Read Adhyaya 24 ↗
Adhyaya 25
The Brahmin's Wife — Faith Tested and Vindicated
A devoted Brahmin's wife undergoes severe testing as her husband is led astray by false counsel to doubt the Guru. She maintains her faith absolutely. The Guru, apparently absent, orchestrates her protection and vindicates her publicly, while the husband's realization deepens his own surrender.
Read Adhyaya 25 ↗
Adhyaya 26
Journey to Gangapur — Arrival at the Bhima-Amaraja Sangam
Narasimha Saraswati arrives at Gangapur (Ganagapura) at the confluence of the Bhima and Amaraja rivers. He recognizes it as the place where Sripada's ashes fell, consecrating it centuries earlier. He announces: "This is Gandharvanagar. I will stay here." His principal 20-year seat is now established.
Read Adhyaya 26 ↗
Adhyaya 27
The Brahmin from Mangalaveda — Liberation
A Brahmin from Mangalaveda arrives at Gangapur carrying severe ancestral sin (Brahmahatya). He prostrates before Narasimha Saraswati and confesses everything. The Guru instructs him in a specific puja, prescribes bathing at the Sangam on certain tithis, and liberates him from all accumulated karma.
Read Adhyaya 27 ↗
Adhyaya 28
The Two Sisters — Devotion and Its Reward
Two devout sisters who serve the Guru community at Gangapur with tireless selfless service are blessed by Narasimha Saraswati. Their devoted service (cooking, cleaning, caring for pilgrims) is declared equal to the highest Vedic rituals. They and their family lineage receive the Guru's permanent grace.
Read Adhyaya 28 ↗
Adhyaya 29
The Blind Man Healed — Sight from Faith
A man blind from birth is brought to Gangapur by his family after years of fruitless pilgrimages. When he falls at the Guru's feet with complete acceptance — not demanding a cure, but surrendering — Narasimha Saraswati touches his eyes. He opens them and sees for the first time, overwhelmed with tears.
Read Adhyaya 29 ↗
Adhyaya 30
The Horse Returns — Miracle of Fidelity
A merchant's prized horse is stolen by bandits. The merchant mourns at Gangapur, praying to Narasimha Saraswati. Days later the horse breaks free from the bandits and finds its way back alone — guided, says the Guru, by its own karma-bond with its devoted owner. Loyalty creates invisible protection.
Read Adhyaya 30 ↗
Adhyaya 31
The Merchant's Son — Freed from Debt and Disgrace
A merchant family's son, ruined by bad debts and social shame, contemplates suicide. Brought to the Guru's feet by his mother, he is told: "Your debts are old karma — come clear." The Guru prescribes a specific worship sequence; within months unexpected money arrives, all debts are cleared, and the family prospers.
Read Adhyaya 31 ↗
Adhyaya 32
The Foolish Brahmin's Son — Transformed to Wisdom
A Brahmin's son, chronically dull and unable to learn despite years of effort, is brought to Gangapur. Narasimha Saraswati places His hand on the boy's head and whispers a mantra. Within months the boy becomes the most learned student in the region — a living demonstration of Guru-shakti awakening latent intelligence.
Read Adhyaya 32 ↗
Adhyaya 33
The Tiger Transformed — Power of the Padukas
A devotee is attacked by a tiger while on pilgrimage. He calls on Narasimha Saraswati and the tiger stops — then circles him and walks away. When the devotee returns and tells the story, the Guru reveals the tiger was a cursed king, now freed by the devotee's involuntary act of Guru-smaran (remembrance).
Read Adhyaya 33 ↗
Adhyaya 34
The Power of Namaskar — Even One Bow Bears Fruit
A casual traveler stops at Gangapur and, following others, makes a half-hearted namaskar (bow) to the Guru. Years later he is saved from a deadly epidemic in his village. Narasimha Saraswati explains to Trivikrama Bharati: "Even the shadow of a Guru's presence protects those who touch it, even unknowingly."
Read Adhyaya 34 ↗
Adhyaya 35
The Scorpion Sting — Healed by Ash
A devoted woman is stung by a scorpion while cooking prasad for the Guru. The venom spreads rapidly. Narasimha Saraswati takes a pinch of ash from His dhuni fire, blows on it, and touches her arm. The swelling vanishes instantly. The sacred ash (vibhuti/udi) from the Guru's fire is established as universal medicine.
Read Adhyaya 35 ↗
Adhyaya 36
The Dying Brahmin — Gift of Extra Life
A Brahmin who is declared by all physicians to be in his final hours is carried to Gangapur. His wife's weeping moves Narasimha Saraswati, who intervenes: "He has service yet to perform." The Brahmin recovers fully over three days. He lives another twenty years in devoted service at the Guru's seat.
Read Adhyaya 36 ↗
Adhyaya 37
The Brahmin Nagnath — Freed from Brahmahatya
The Brahmin Nagnath, haunted by the karma of an accidental killing of a Brahmin in his past life, can find no peace despite decades of penance. At Gangapur, Narasimha Saraswati reveals the past-life cause, prescribes the precise remedy, and absolves him completely — establishing that only Guru grace can dissolve the deepest karma.
Read Adhyaya 37 ↗
Adhyaya 38
The King's Minister — Grace Across All Stations
A powerful royal minister arrives at Gangapur with great pomp. Narasimha Saraswati receives him as an equal — not as a dignitary, but as a soul in search of liberation. The minister's pride is gently dissolved as the Guru demonstrates that rank has no meaning in the Guru's presence.
Read Adhyaya 38 ↗
Adhyaya 39
The Cloth Merchant's Miracle — Gratitude Returned
A cloth merchant who years earlier had donated a piece of cloth to Narasimha Saraswati (not knowing who He was) is now in business ruin. The Guru recognizes him and says: "You gave to me; I give back." A miraculous sequence of circumstances restores his fortune. The law of seva and return is illustrated.
Read Adhyaya 39 ↗
Adhyaya 40
The Dead Man Raised at Gangapur
A devotee dies suddenly of fever while on pilgrimage to Gangapur. His grief-stricken wife refuses to leave the body from the Guru's presence. Narasimha Saraswati, sitting in meditation, opens His eyes after three days and says simply: "Get up." The man rises, fully recovered, to the awe of hundreds of witnesses.
Read Adhyaya 40 ↗
Adhyaya 41
The Seven Disciples — Tests of Absolute Obedience
Seven devoted disciples are given apparently impossible or absurd instructions by the Guru as tests of pure obedience. Those who obey without question receive liberation; those who hesitate or question reveal their remaining attachment. The teaching: Guru's command, however strange, is always for the disciple's highest good.
Read Adhyaya 41 ↗
Adhyaya 42
The Brahmin from the South — Healed of Incurable Disease
A Brahmin scholar travels from the far south, afflicted with an incurable wasting disease. He had read the Gurucharitra and came to Gangapur with one-pointed faith. Narasimha Saraswati receives him and says: "Your reading of the text has already healed you — come and confirm it." The man is completely well.
Read Adhyaya 42 ↗
Adhyaya 43
Omniscience — The Guru Knows All Minds
Several anecdotes establish Narasimha Saraswati's complete omniscience — He reveals the secret thoughts and distant actions of devotees who have never spoken to Him. When asked how, He says: "All minds are open to me as the sky is open to the bird. There is no distance in the Guru's awareness."
Read Adhyaya 43 ↗
Adhyaya 44
Saraswati Gangadhara — The Author Receives the Command
Saraswati Gangadhara, the author of the Gurucharitra, describes his own direct experience of the Guru's command in a vision: "Compose my life story in Marathi verse so that all My devotees — not only Sanskrit scholars — may access this nectar." This explains both the origin and the purpose of the Gurucharitra text.
Read Adhyaya 44 ↗
Adhyaya 45
The Seven-Day Parayan — Rules and Procedure
Narasimha Saraswati prescribes the rules for the Saptah Parayan (seven-day reading) of the Gurucharitra: fasting, purity, the specific chapters to be read on each day, the ideal tithis, and the rituals to perform before and after. This practice is said to cleanse all sins and grant all four goals of life.
Read Adhyaya 45 ↗
Adhyaya 46
The Significance of Each Chapter Day
The specific spiritual fruits of reading the Gurucharitra on each of the seven parayan days are enumerated in detail: Day 1 removes ignorance; Day 2 cures disease; Day 3 dissolves debt; Day 4 grants offspring; Day 5 brings spiritual power; Day 6 liberates from ancestral curses; Day 7 grants moksha.
Read Adhyaya 46 ↗
Adhyaya 47
The Padukas — Living Presence of the Guru
Narasimha Saraswati explains the supreme significance of the Guru's padukas (sandals). "My padukas are not shoes — they are my living form. Worship them as you would worship me." The padukas installed at Gangapur, Narsoba Wadi, and other seats are permanent living shrines of the Guru's presence.
Read Adhyaya 47 ↗
Adhyaya 48
Gandharvanagar — The Cosmic Identity of Gangapur
Narasimha Saraswati reveals Gangapur's cosmic identity as Gandharvanagar (city of celestial musicians) — a place where the Gandharvas, Siddhas, and divine beings constantly worship. He declares that Sripada Srivallabha's ashes consecrated this land centuries earlier: "This is my seat from before time."
Read Adhyaya 48 ↗
Adhyaya 49
Farewell Discourse — Shri Guru's Final Instructions
Narasimha Saraswati gathers His closest devotees and disciples for a final teaching. He instructs them on how to live, serve, and maintain the Guru's seat after His departure. His core instruction: "Serve the poor. Worship the cow. Honor parents. Read the Gurucharitra. I will always be with you."
Read Adhyaya 49 ↗
Adhyaya 50
Departure for Kardali Vana — The Last Journey
Narasimha Saraswati announces His departure from Gangapur toward Kardali Vana (the Banana Forest) near Srisailam. Devotees weep. He reassures them: "I am not leaving. My presence at Gangapur is eternal. Come here with faith and you will see me." He walks away and is never seen in physical form again.
Read Adhyaya 50 ↗
Adhyaya 51
Phala Shruti — The Fruits of Reading
The specific spiritual fruits of reading or hearing the Gurucharitra are enumerated in full: removal of all sins, cure of incurable diseases, freedom from debt and ancestral curses, success in all endeavours, fulfillment of desires, and ultimately liberation from the cycle of birth and death.
Read Adhyaya 51 ↗
Adhyaya 52
Conclusion — The Eternal Presence of Shri Guru
The Gurucharitra concludes with the declaration of Narasimha Saraswati's eternal living presence at Gangapur. The final benediction: "Whoever reads this text with faith and devotion will never face poverty, disease, or misery. In their darkest hour, the Guru Himself will appear to rescue them." Shri Guru Datta.
Read Adhyaya 52 ↗
Key Persons — Shri Gurucharitra
60+ persons
✍️
Saraswati Gangadhara
Author
Direct disciple of Narasimha Saraswati. Composed the Gurucharitra in Marathi Ovibaddha (verse) on the Guru's direct command, so all devotees could access the divine story.
🧘
Namdharak
Devotee / Narrator
The devoted seeker whose questions to Siddhamuni form the frame of the entire Gurucharitra. His longing for the Guru's presence and guidance represents every sincere devotee.
📿
Siddhamuni
Teacher / Narrator
The disciple of Saraswati Gangadhara who narrates the Gurucharitra to Namdharak. The dialogue between Siddhamuni and Namdharak forms the structural backbone of the entire text.
🔱
Narasimha Saraswati
Divine Subject — 2nd Datta Avatar
Second incarnation of Dattatreya in Kali Yuga. Born 1378 CE at Lad Karanja (Karanjnagari), Maharashtra. Main seat: Ganagapura (Karnataka). Departed from Kardali Vana near Srisailam in 1459 CE. Prophesied by Sripada Srivallabha.
👨
Madhava Sharma
Family — Father
Father of Narasimha Saraswati. A Brahmin householder from Karanjnagar, Maharashtra. He passed away during Narasimha Saraswati's childhood, contributing to His renunciation of home life.
👩
Amba
Family — Mother
Mother of Narasimha Saraswati. A pious Brahmin woman of deep faith. After Madhava Sharma's death she raised her son alone. She witnessed the early manifestations of her son's divine nature.
🧘
Krishnananda Saraswati
Guru — Sanyas Diksha at Kashi
The sanyasi at Kashi (Varanasi) who gave Narasimha Saraswati his sanyas initiation (diksha). He gave Him the name Narasimha Saraswati. This completes the cosmic chain: Sripada → Narasimha Saraswati.
📚
Trivikrama Bharati
Devotee — Former Scholar
A renowned Vedic scholar who challenged Narasimha Saraswati and was utterly defeated. He fell at the Guru's feet and became one of His closest disciples, embodying the transition from intellectual pride to Guru bhakti.
🙏
Brahmin Ambarisha
Devotee — Key Episode
His son died of snakebite. Ambarisha's wife's absolute faith moved Narasimha Saraswati to bring the dead boy back to life. The most famous miracle of the Gurucharitra.
🌿
The Leper Brahmin
Devotee — Healed
A Brahmin suffering from severe leprosy who surrendered completely at Narasimha Saraswati's feet. After bathing in the sacred waters blessed by the Guru, his leprosy was entirely cured.
👁️
The Blind Beggar
Devotee — Healed
A blind beggar who had served the Guru's devotees faithfully for years. He received the Guru's direct blessing and his sight was restored. Illustrates: service to devotees = service to the Guru.
🔱
Swami Samarth of Akkalkot
Future Avatar — Prophesied
The third Dattatreya avatar, prophesied in the Gurucharitra as the future appearance of Narasimha Saraswati (just as Sripada prophesied Narasimha Saraswati). Appeared in Akkalkot (Prajnapura), Maharashtra, in the 19th century.
🔱
Dattatreya
Cosmic Source — Adi Guru
The divine origin of the entire Datta Parampara. Son of Atri Maharishi and Anasuya. Combined form of Brahma, Vishnu, and Shiva. All avatars — Sripada, Narasimha Saraswati, Swami Samarth — are His re-manifestations in Kali Yuga. The Gurucharitra opens with His story and traces His cosmic lineage.
🌟
Atri Maharishi
Sage — Father of Dattatreya
One of the seven Saptarishis (great sages). His long tapas (austerities) and his wife Anasuya's purity together drew Brahma, Vishnu, and Shiva to incarnate as their son Dattatreya. The Gurucharitra begins by recounting Atri's lineage, establishing the cosmic pedigree of the Datta tradition.
🌸
Anasuya
Divine Mother — of Dattatreya
Wife of Atri Maharishi. The epitome of purity and marital fidelity (Pativrata). When Brahma, Vishnu, and Shiva came disguised to test her, she turned them into infants — demonstrating her spiritual power. They then permanently incarnated as Dattatreya (Vishnu-amsha), Soma (Brahma-amsha), and Durvasa (Shiva-amsha).
🙏
Wife of Brahmin Ambarisha
Devotee — The Faith That Moved a Mountain
The unnamed wife whose absolute, unshakeable faith is the real subject of the most famous miracle in the Gurucharitra. While Ambarisha was devastated, she remained calm, surrendered the dead boy to the Guru, and waited. It was her faith — not her grief — that moved Narasimha Saraswati to restore life. The Gurucharitra names her faith as the decisive force.
🌹
The Brahmin Widow of Ganagapura
Devotee — Miraculous Restoration
A young Brahmin widow whose husband had died suddenly. In absolute despair she carried his body to Narasimha Saraswati and refused to leave. Narasimha Saraswati, moved by her total surrender, restored her husband to life. The episode establishes: the Guru's grace transcends even death when the devotee's surrender is complete.
👶
The Childless Brahmin Couple
Devotees — Blessed with a Child
A devout Brahmin couple who had been childless for many years. After sustained worship and service at Ganagapura, Narasimha Saraswati blessed them with a son. Their story — in multiple chapters — establishes Ganagapura as a place where impossible desires become fulfilled through Guru bhakti combined with sincere service.
🧘
The Nishkama (Desireless) Devotee
Devotee — Pure Bhakti
A Brahmin who came to Narasimha Saraswati with no personal request — no desire for health, wealth, or progeny — only for the Guru's grace and liberation. His detachment moved Narasimha Saraswati deeply. Used as the Gurucharitra's model of Nishkama Bhakti (devotion without desire) — the highest form of surrender.
🐦
The Brahmin Reborn as a Crow
Devotee — Liberation from Rebirth
A Brahmin who, due to past karma, had been reborn as a crow. The crow approached Narasimha Saraswati, displayed unusual reverence, and received His blessing — immediately being liberated from that animal birth and ascending to higher realms. Illustrates the Guru's capacity to liberate even beings in non-human forms.
📿
Pingala Naga
Devotee — Ganagapura
A devoted Brahmin at Ganagapura who served Narasimha Saraswati faithfully. Associated with key episodes of service and teachings at Ganagapura. His steadfast daily service became the model for how a householder-devotee should approach the Guru — through consistent, ego-free service rather than dramatic acts.
🧓
The Old Brahmin from Vasai
Devotee — Long-Distance Journey
An elderly Brahmin from Vasai (near present-day Mumbai) who undertook an arduous journey to reach Narasimha Saraswati at Ganagapura. Despite old age and physical difficulty, his faith carried him. His story establishes the principle: the Guru's grace reaches across any distance when the devotee's longing is genuine and complete.
🐄
Kamadhenu (Sacred Cow)
Divine Being — Cosmic Episode
The divine wish-fulfilling cow of the celestial realm. Appears in the Gurucharitra's cosmological sections explaining Dattatreya's nature and the abundance that flows through Guru bhakti. The cow's milk is used in key ritual contexts, and the relationship between the Guru and Kamadhenu symbolises unlimited divine grace available to devotees.
📜
Yajnavalkya
Sage — Referenced in Teachings
The great Vedic sage and philosopher, author of the Brihadaranyaka Upanishad and codifier of the Yajnavalkya Smriti. The Gurucharitra extensively cites his philosophy of Brahman realization in the chapters on Vedantic teaching. Narasimha Saraswati quotes Yajnavalkya to explain the nature of Atman, Brahman, and liberation.
🕉️
Adi Shankaracharya
Historical — Vedantic Reference
The great 8th-century Advaita Vedanta philosopher and reformer. His works — Vivekachudamani, Bhaja Govindam, Shankaracharya Bhashya on the Upanishads — are cited throughout the Gurucharitra's teaching chapters. Narasimha Saraswati frequently quotes Shankara's formulations to explain non-dual consciousness and the nature of Maya.
🌿
The Leper Brahmin's Wife
Devotee — Healed Alongside Husband
The wife of the leper Brahmin who accompanied her husband to Narasimha Saraswati. Her own body had developed symptoms of leprosy through years of caring for her husband. Both were bathed in sacred waters and both were healed. The episode emphasises: the grace given to a devotee extends to those who serve and love that devotee.
🛕
The Brahmin of Tryambakeshvara
Devotee — Teachings at the Jyotirlinga
A Brahmin devotee who encountered Narasimha Saraswati at Tryambakeshvara (near Nashik). He became one of the first devotees to receive extended private teachings from Narasimha Saraswati on the nature of the Guru, the Brahman, and the path of liberation. Some scholars identify this episode as the seed of the Gurucharitra's most profound philosophical chapters.
📚
Kumaravyasa
Historical — Medieval Kannada Poet
The great 15th-century Kannada poet Gadugina Naranappa, known as Kumaravyasa. Contemporary of Narasimha Saraswati. Author of the celebrated Gadugina Bharata (Kannada Mahabharata). Some traditions connect him to Narasimha Saraswati's circle. His literary output flourished in the same Karnataka-Deccan region where Narasimha Saraswati established His seat at Ganagapura.
🐘
Ganesha (Invocation)
Divine Being — Opening Invocation
Every adhyaya (chapter) of the Gurucharitra traditionally begins with an invocation to Ganesha — the remover of obstacles and the Lord of beginnings. The Gurucharitra is structurally a parampara text: Atri → Dattatreya → Sripada → Narasimha Saraswati → Saraswati Gangadhara → Siddhamuni → Namdharak. Ganesha's presence at each chapter opening consecrates the entire transmission.
🎵
Saraswati Devi (Invocation)
Divine Being — Goddess of Knowledge
The goddess of learning, speech, and the arts. Invoked at the opening of the Gurucharitra alongside Ganesha. The text explicitly says Saraswati Gangadhara composed the Gurucharitra under Her direct blessing — the words of the text are described as Her direct speech flowing through the author. The name Saraswati appears in both the author's name and in Narasimha Saraswati's own name.
🪷
Brahma
Divine Being — Creator
One of the Hindu Trinity. Part of the cosmic backdrop of the Gurucharitra — the same Brahma who, with Vishnu and Shiva, incarnated as the three sons of Anasuya, is now present (in His Dattatreya-amsha form) as Narasimha Saraswati. The Gurucharitra's theological chapters extensively discuss Brahma's role in creation and the cosmic cycle.
🌀
Vishnu
Divine Being — Preserver
The Preserver of the universe; the Vishnu-amsha is primary in Dattatreya's composite form. The Gurucharitra recounts His twelve avatars extensively in its early chapters, culminating in the declaration that Dattatreya — and Narasimha Saraswati — represents the living Vishnu-principle operating through the Guru in Kali Yuga.
Sacred Places — Shri Gurucharitra
20+ places
⭐
Ganagapura (Gangapur)
Primary Seat — Karnataka
Principal seat of Narasimha Saraswati for ~20 years. At the confluence of the Bhima and Amaraja rivers, Gulbarga district, Karnataka. Revealed as Gandharvanagar — city of celestial musicians. Sripada's ashes fell here, consecrating it centuries earlier.
